Well, um... I suppose my appetite has been a little off. I hate to even mention it, because I know there are folks out there who don't have enough to eat, and I certainly don't want to sound ungrateful for a good hot meal.

Well, um... I mean, I don't think I'm a bad person or anything. I try really hard to be a good neighbor and a good employee. I always show up for my shifts, you know? I don't want you to think I'm the kind of guy who just gives up or doesn't care.

Well, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I've had days where I felt a little blue, sure—doesn't everyone? But nothing that lasted for weeks on end or made it hard to get out of bed.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... um... I mean, I try really hard to keep up with everything. I don't want to sound like I'm just letting everything slide, because I do care. I really do. 

But... I guess lately, things have gotten a little harder to juggle. I, um, I fell behind on a couple of bills recently. And I missed a few callbacks and appointments. It's embarrassing to admit, honestly. I usually pride myself on being on top of things. I've even been trying to look for some extra work, but I just haven't been able to keep a steady job search going. 

And at home... well, my usual routines have just gotten a bit sloppy. I hate to say it, but it got to the point where I realized I couldn't just push through it with willpower anymore. I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or irresponsible. I'm really trying my best to get back on track. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest with you about how things have been going.

I just kind of keep to myself and do what needs doing. I hope that's the right answer? I just kind of keep to myself and do what needs doing. I hope that's the right answer? I wouldn't want to make a habit of feeling this way. I usually just try to look on the bright side and keep busy at the store. \n\nSo, no, I don't think I've ever had a stretch like this before. I hope that's helpful? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right history.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period lasting two weeks or more when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had trouble functioning—before this current three-month period?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't think so. I don't know, I just kind of quit caring about them. They started feeling more like chores than anything fun. I feel bad even saying that, because I know I should be keeping busy and enjoying my hobbies. I just haven't had the energy for it. Is that normal when you're feeling a bit down like this? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, over these past few months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I suppose so. I mean, I really don't like to use words like \"hopeless,\" because I know things could always be worse, right? I try to keep things in perspective. But... after that sort of burst of energy I had a few months ago finally wore off, I just felt like... like somebody had pulled the plug on me. \n\nI'd just sit at the kitchen table in the mornings, staring at my coffee, and I just felt so... empty. And honestly, a little ashamed that I couldn't just snap out of it and be productive. I should be able to just shake it off, you know? I hope it doesn't make me sound weak, admitting that I've been feeling that way most days. I really am trying my best to push through it. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a loss of energy most of the day, nearly every day, over these past few months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe I've been a little tired? I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. I still go to work and everything, you know? I really try to pull my weight at the store. \n\nBut... yeah, I suppose I haven't had quite the same pep in my step. It's just a little harder to get going in the mornings, and by the time my shift is over, I'm pretty wiped out. I feel bad even calling it fatigue, because I know getting older means you slow down a bit anyway. I'm 54, so maybe it's just my age catching up with me? I hope I'm not making a big deal out of nothing... does that sound like the kind of energy loss you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose my appetite has been a little off. I hate to even mention it, because I know there are folks out there who don't have enough to eat, and I certainly don't want to sound ungrateful for a good hot meal. \n\nBut... yeah, most days lately I actually have to remind myself to eat. I'll get home from the store, and supper just... it just sits there on the plate looking like too much trouble, you know? I just kind of pick at it. I haven't really been trying to lose any weight, but I did notice my jeans are definitely fitting a bit looser than they used to. \n\nI feel a little embarrassed admitting that I can't even finish a normal plate of food right now. I should be taking better care of myself. I hope I'm not making a big deal out of just skipping a few meals... does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety for no clear reason?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I hope I haven't been moving too slowly at work—I try really hard to keep up with the younger folks at the store, you know? I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm slacking off. \n\nAnd as for being restless... no, not really. I try to stay pretty steady. I don't want to be a bother to anyone by pacing around or fidgeting. I just kind of keep to myself and do what needs doing. I hope that's the right answer? I just haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough, or that you’ve let people down—even if you can’t quite put your finger on why?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I don't think I'm a bad person or anything. I try really hard to be a good neighbor and a good employee. I always show up for my shifts, you know? I don't want you to think I'm the kind of guy who just gives up or doesn't care.\n\nBut... I guess sometimes I do wonder if I could be doing more. Like maybe I'm not pulling my weight as much as I should be, especially since I've been so tired lately. I hate the thought of letting my manager down, or the customers. It's just a little nagging feeling, though. I'm sure everyone feels that way sometimes, right? Just wanting to do a better job? I hope I don't sound like I'm throwing a pity party for myself. I just try to do my best, and I hope that's enough.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt this way for several weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I've had days where I felt a little blue, sure—doesn't everyone? But nothing that lasted for weeks on end or made it hard to get out of bed. \n\nI've always prided myself on being pretty reliable. I just put my head down and keep going, you know? I wouldn't want to make a habit of feeling this way. I usually just try to look on the bright side and keep busy at the store. \n\nSo, no, I don't think I've ever had a stretch like this before. I hope that's helpful? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right history.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period lasting two weeks or more when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had trouble functioning—before this current three-month period?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't think so. I mean, I know you just asked something kind of similar, and I really hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression or anything. But honestly, no. Before these last few months, I've always been pretty steady. \n\nI've always been the kind of guy who just gets up and goes to work, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I've had a history of... well, of falling apart or not being able to handle my responsibilities. I've always tried my best to be someone people can count on. So, no, nothing that lasted two weeks or stopped me from doing what I needed to do. Is... is it okay that I don't have anything else to tell you about that? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty carrying out your usual daily responsibilities at work or home because of how you’ve been feeling?\n\nPatient: Well... um... I mean, I try really hard to keep up with everything. I don't want to sound like I'm just letting everything slide, because I do care. I really do. \n\nBut... I guess lately, things have gotten a little harder to juggle. I, um, I fell behind on a couple of bills recently. And I missed a few callbacks and appointments. It's embarrassing to admit, honestly. I usually pride myself on being on top of things. I've even been trying to look for some extra work, but I just haven't been able to keep a steady job search going. \n\nAnd at home... well, my usual routines have just gotten a bit sloppy. I hate to say it, but it got to the point where I realized I couldn't just push through it with willpower anymore. I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or irresponsible. I'm really trying my best to get back on track. Does that make sense? I just want to be honest with you about how things have been going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood is almost entirely flat or empty, and that you don't feel better even when something good happens, like seeing friends or enjoying a hobby?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I always try to appreciate the good things, you know? I don't want to sound ungrateful or like I'm ignoring my friends when they try to cheer me up. But... yeah, I guess empty is a good word for it. \n\nAfter this, um, this big burst of energy I had a while back wore off, it honestly just felt like somebody had completely pulled the plug on me. I would just sit at the kitchen table, staring at my coffee... just feeling totally empty. And, honestly, really ashamed that I couldn't just snap out of it and be happy like I'm supposed to be. Even when nice things happen now, I just can't seem to feel them. \n\nI hope it's okay to admit that to you? I really don't want to come across as a downer or like I'm not trying. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt down, lost interest in things, or had trouble functioning for two weeks or more, even if it got better on its own?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I think we might have touched on this just a little bit ago? I really hope I'm not misunderstanding your question, or, you know, making things difficult for your notes. I definitely don't want to be unhelpful. \n\nBut... no, I really can't think of any other time in my life before this current stretch where I felt that way for two whole weeks. I mean, everyone has a bad day here and there, sure, but nothing that ever stopped me from functioning or going into the store. I've always just put on my name tag, gone out to the sales floor, and kept a smile on for the customers, no matter what. \n\nSo, no, nothing like that in the past, even things that got better on their own. I'm sorry if I don't have a more detailed answer for you on that one... is it alright that my answer is still just a no? I really am trying to be as thorough as I can for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of depression before this current one—like feeling down, losing interest in things, or having trouble functioning—that lasted for two weeks or more, and then fully went away for at least two months before coming back again?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... gosh, I hope I didn't explain things poorly a minute ago. I feel terrible if I'm making you ask almost the same thing twice, I really don't want to be a difficult patient for you. \n\nBut... yeah, just like I was saying, I really haven't ever had a time like that before this current one. Not even one that went away for a couple of months and then came back. I've always been pretty steady, just doing my shifts and keeping my head up. \n\nI'm sorry, I know it's basically the same answer I just gave... is it alright that I don't have anything like that in my past to report? I really am trying to give you exactly what you need for your evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of depression before this one — like feeling down, losing interest in things, or having trouble functioning — that lasted for two weeks or more, and then completely went away for at least two months before coming back again?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I really must be doing a terrible job explaining myself today. I am so sorry, Doctor. I feel like I keep making you ask the exact same thing, and I really don't want to waste your time or frustrate you. \n\nI promise I'm thinking really, really hard about it. But... no, honestly, before this recent spell, I never had anything like that. No two-week periods of feeling down that went away and came back. I was always just regular old me, going to work at the store and doing my best to be a good employee. \n\nAm I answering these the right way for you? I can try to dig deeper if you think I'm missing something, or if I'm not giving you the kind of details you need. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of depression before this one — like feeling down, losing interest in things, or having trouble functioning — that lasted for two weeks or more, and then completely went away for at least two months before coming back again?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm really letting you down here. You've asked me this a few times now, and I must be giving you the wrong kind of answer, or maybe I'm just not making any sense at all. I really don't mean to be difficult, I promise. \n\nBut... no, sir. I really haven't. I swear I've racked my brain, and I just can't think of any time before this where I was down like that for a couple of weeks, got better for a few months, and then had it happen again. I've always just been... well, fine, you know? Just going in, folding the shirts, helping the customers. \n\nI feel awful that I don't have a better answer for you. Is there a different way I should be thinking about this? I really want to give you what you need for your paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of depression before this one — like feeling down, losing interest in things, or having trouble functioning — that lasted for two weeks or more, and then completely went away for at least two months before coming back again?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... Doctor, I am so, so sorry. I must be doing something terribly wrong, aren't I? You keep having to ask me the exact same question, and I feel just awful that I'm not giving you what you're looking for. I really don't want to be a bother or make your job harder.\n\nI'm trying so hard to think of something, anything, just to be helpful. But... no, I really haven't. Before all this started recently, I never had a time where I was depressed for a few weeks, then got better, and then got depressed again. I was always just... normal, I guess. Just going to work, doing my best to be reliable. \n\nAm I ruining your evaluation by not having a different answer? Please tell me if I need to explain it differently. I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
